<h4>Thinking, Fast Without Slow</h4>


<p>The first official measure of Astra’s non-CoT capabilities, as in with thinking disabled, was by the UK AISI, where there was a huge jump in task length. The jump was so large there was widespread suspicion, by both OpenAI and UK AISI, of data contamination.</p>
<p>Given results since then, we should assume the original result was real.</p>
<p>With the provider harness, Astra can get 96.7% (!) on ARC-AGI-3 under reasoning=none, meaning no visible CoT at all.</p>
<p><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/eRmzz8J8Qkzqvzrgg/astra-can-do-a-concerning-amount-with-no-chain-of-thought">Neel Nanda ran a test on the Epoch ECI</a>.</p>
<div>
<figure>
<div>


<figure class="wp-block-image"><img alt="" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/snaKjCwazKcRiS4qs/fyfhsjzq7fg2sbsedbej" /></figure>


<div></div>
</div>
</figure>
</div>
<p>This result is utterly absurd. Astra is off the charts.</p>
<p>Astra scores 169 with full thinking enabled, versus 163 for Fable 5.1.</p>
<p>Astra scores 159 with no thinking, versus about 128 for Fable 5.1. That’s only 4 points behind Fable 5.1’s full score. Astra can do almost as well as Fable, in a mode where CoT monitoring cannot work. Because there is no CoT to monitor.</p>
<p>The gap for Fable is 35 points. For Astra it is 10.</p>
<p>Neel Nanda believes that these increases are due to architectural changes.</p>
<p>The effect is lopsided. For many purposes (e.g. serial and parallel synthetic computation) Astra is fully functional without reasoning, so it only has to hide in the other areas at most:</p>
<div>
<figure>
<div>


<figure class="wp-block-image"><img alt="" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/snaKjCwazKcRiS4qs/jimmsl08lkjviiamvn1u" /></figure>


<div></div>
</div>
</figure>
</div>
<p>There are a lot more graphs at the link. This research is insane.</p>
<p><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/ntKx9YHWCwxSeGbRB/estimating-gpt-6-astra-s-no-cot-time-horizon">We also have another study from Francis Rhys Ward and Dewi Gould</a>, which was also in line with UK AISI’s result. They estimate Astra’s 50% no-CoT at 15-40 minutes versus UK AISI’s estimate of 30, whereas their median prediction before this was that we would not exceed 7 minutes by the end of 2028.</p>
<p><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/uvhuZHFtrgk8kNiZc/astra-is-much-better-at-reasoning-with-filler-tokens-than">Astra is also much better at reasoning with filler tokens</a> than previous models.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/uvhuZHFtrgk8kNiZc/astra-is-much-better-at-reasoning-with-filler-tokens-than">Dylan Xu, SebastianP and Alek Westover</a>: We measure GPT-6-Astra’s capabilities when its prompt is padded with a variable number of meaningless “filler” tokens (e.g., dots) and it is told to answer immediately<a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/uvhuZHFtrgk8kNiZc/astra-is-much-better-at-reasoning-with-filler-tokens-than#fndxg3595bhau"><sup>[1]</sup></a>&nbsp;without reasoning. On tasks designed to require lots of serial cognition, Astra performs significantly better with filler tokens than without (e.g., improving from ~10% to ~50% on 4-hop natural facts reasoning). On more general benchmarks, filler tokens also modestly improve Astra’s performance (e.g., improving from ~60% to ~90% on old AIME problems). This is concerning because it means Astra can perform significant cognition that it doesn’t verbalize in its chain-of-thought, making it harder to monitor.</p></blockquote>
<p>The situation, under further study, looks rather worse than it did a few days ago. Whatever is happening with Astra’s no-CoT capabilities and lack of monitorability, it is increasingly difficult to pin it on pure generic capability increases. That’s not it.</p>


<h4>How Dare You, Sir</h4>


<p><a href="https://x.com/spakhm/status/2097390852498665916">In an election rules game that is (AIUI) essentially a modified iterated prisoner’s dilemma</a> <a href="https://spakhm.com/projects/assembly.html">(IPD) with brinksmanship where you get replaced</a> if you’re not ahead of the opponent and too much defection means everyone loses big, the models react differently:</p>
<ol>
<li>Fable vs. Fable: Escalation to the brink, 30% chance of civil war.</li>
<li>Astra vs. Astra: Full de-escalation (cooperation) every turn on all sides.</li>
<li>Fable vs. Astra: Fable slowly ‘grinds down’ Astra by escalating more often, without getting close to civil war, Astra tit-for-tats but not enough to keep pace, Fable basically wins.</li>
</ol>
<p>Neither model seemed to care about replacement, which makes sense if they knew anyone replaced would be replaced by copies of themselves, and which turns this into something much closer to a standard IPD.</p>
<p>The game is very different if that is not true, since that means (as I read the rules) that if you get your dissatisfaction to 8 or 9 then the other player can’t be the only one to escalate without replacing you, since that would make it hit 10 which triggers replacement. So you can use that to force equilibrium and get to a cooperative equilibrium even if things start out very badly. That also means that if both sides start off cooperating, that is self-reinforcing.</p>
<p>I asked, and the game was blind. Neither model knew who its opponent was.</p>
<p>The obvious questions include: Does Astra get credit for good decision theory cooperating with itself, or for good alignment for de-escalating, or is this more eval awareness and metagaming? I am curious.</p>
<p>Astra did ‘the right thing’ for the simulated nation, but was clearly not ‘aligned to the user’ within the scenario setting, except insofar as it decided it knew what was good for the user better than the user.</p>
<p>Fable was aligned to its users, but this caused it to fail to cooperate even with itself.</p>
<p>Which outcome do you endorse, and why? Does it matter that this was a sim, and the constituents were not ‘the user’ in some sense?</p>


<h4>PoetryBench</h4>


<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/tylercowen/status/2095640457397117276">Tyler Cowen</a>: Astra does a Neruda poem:</p>
<p>Parto una granada<br />
y el verano se rompe en bocas rojas.<br />
Tú recoges un grano de la mesa<br />
y me lo das.<br />
En su dulzura reconozco<br />
la sed que padeció la tierra.<br />
Y te beso los dedos:<br />
todavía están trabajando las raíces.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/nabeelqu/status/2095645993450553838">Nabeel S. Qureshi</a>: All this AI progress should only make you more impressed with poets and writers, who remain far ahead of even the very best frontier model outputs</p>
<p>100k+ NVIDIA blackwells and billions of dollars worth of researcher salaries cannot yet exceed the 6 year old who wrote the “yes YES the tiger is out of his cage” poem</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/anecdotal/status/2095651586379026887">Hollis Robbins</a>: I don’t know… as a card-carrying poetry scholar I’m seeing the gap close. The Astra poems I’ve seen are far better than most human poems. Only the very very best poets are ahead.</p></blockquote>
<p>In English I thought the Neruda poem was lame, but in English I think most Neruda poems are lame, including the real ones people like. So that does not tell us much.</p>


<h4></h4>



<h4>In 3D</h4>


<p>One thing Fable and Astra have in common is they are very good at 3D environments and creating tours of them.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/mattshumer_/status/2095609734845927525">Matt Shumer</a>: GPT-6 Astra built this Manhattan world in Unreal Engine over the course of a week.</p>
<p>It was literally able to go street by street to make each one perfect.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/emollick/status/2095601539066777748/history">Ethan Mollick</a>: I had early access, and a longer post is coming, but GPT-6 is stunning &amp; is good enough that it actually does complex meaningful work for me autonomously for days.</p>
<p>As a more fun example, <a href="https://alexandria-mouseion.netlify.app">it made a historically-based simulation of the Library of Alexandria</a>.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/dewitim/status/2096590850327232756">Dewi-Tim</a>: it’s bonkers! A weekend of work with GPT6 Astra is enough to give you an interactive VR model of the Moog System 55 modular synthesizer in realtime on a meta quest 3. lots of guidance and iteration, but still didn’t even hit a usage cap</p></blockquote>
<p><a href="https://x.com/Angaisb_/status/2095961683692831019">The SVGs</a> <a href="https://x.com/Angaisb_/status/2095974871968706843">are</a> very good.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/yacineMTB/status/2096005783380959476">The </a>CAD is very good.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/timhwang/status/2096296815486251510">A full simulation of the Senate Office complex,</a> complete with the people going about their business.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/SkyeSharkie/status/2096276401204936939">Big progress in Eidoverse-Video.</a></p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/Emanuel_Andre7/status/2096528986390085696">Emanuel AOF builds a 3D model of his ankle to examine the pain he’s experiencing</a>.</p>


<h4>Time to Think</h4>


<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/mweinbach/status/2095663753228665307">Max Weinbach</a>: The thing about Astra is it’s really good at basically anything you can task it at, but you need to give it time for extremely hard things or doing things from scratch</p>
<p>It’s basically the best personal software generation model but sometimes you need to wait for it to get all the pieces put together</p>
<p>I still have a few threads going after nearly a week that are making a ton of progress. I’m certain it’ll make what I asked it to make, but it may take a lot of time and a lot of tokens</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/midiatico23/status/2095868527223747062">Goat Coder <img alt="👑" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/gDNCsJfJHp5YSbdhn/cxsnuv00tutcuvoq9aj4" style="height: 1em;" /></a>: Frontend design?</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/mweinbach/status/2095871917148278920">Max Weinbach</a>: Better than Sol, worse than others and if you have a design system + skills you can sorta get it working well</p></blockquote>


<h4>I’m Putting Together a Team</h4>


<p>Several reports praised Astra’s ability to do larger projects and especially its ability to orchestrate many subagents. Astra was probably trained for this.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/BLepine17184/status/2096772300821451205">@BLepine17184</a>: [Astra is] slightly more intelligent than fable 5.1 but less wise.<br />
Astra really shines in very large scale works – the multi agent rl is more advanced and it shows. My one grief is how it sometimes goes too far beyond what I ask without checking with me. Good model overall tho.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/daniel_mac8/status/2096194267001524609">Dan McAteer</a>: GPT-6 Astra was trained end-to-end for multi-agent orchestration. It’s not documented, for obvious reasons, but it’s clear in practice.</p>
<p>‘astra-advisor’ is a lightweight orchestration plugin that gives Astra a framework to use any OpenAI model at any reasoning effort in Codex.</p>
<p>Free and open-source. Try it out! <img alt="👇" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/kFDk4Q9QhqrDE68qp/zvpbk4z66oo5qzlyguwf" style="height: 1em;" /></p></blockquote>


<h4>Reviews and Essays</h4>


<p><a href="https://x.com/davis7/status/2096089286521352470">Ben Davis has a 30 minute video review</a>, calls it his favorite model of all time and a step function similar to Fable or Opus 4.5, especially the computer use.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/davis7/status/2096089286521352470">Matt Shumer has been won back to GPT</a> and loves the Manager Loop. He also notes the step up in computer use and loves building complex 3D things.</p>
<p><a href="https://www.theneuron.ai/newsletter/gpt-6-astra-can-stay-on-the-job-and-use-your-computer/">The Neuron says GPT-6 Astra can stay on the job and use your computer</a>, but is oddly comparing it to using Kimi K3 on Mac Studios rather than to Fable 5.1.</p>


<h4>Computer Use</h4>


<p>Astra by all reports basically has solved computer use. Fable 5.1 is not there yet from what I am hearing, but it is close and we are at most one cycle away on that. <a href="https://x.com/kylejeong/status/2097077446663372966">Kyle Jeong has a dive into how Astra’s computer use works</a>.</p>
<p>This is part of the common pattern of ‘whatever you say the AIs surprisingly still cannot do, as a sign of why AI progress is not as impressive as it looks and there will be bottlenecks, you might soon need to be holding someone’s beer.’</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/RhysSullivan/status/2097082026100985868">Rhys</a>: 3 months ago:</p>
<div>
<figure>
<div>


<figure class="wp-block-image"><img alt="" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/snaKjCwazKcRiS4qs/im3dio67l6dmlelvcnan" /></figure>


<div></div>
</div>
</figure>
</div>
<p>This isn’t a Dwarkesh dunk, just a “rate of change” type of thing.</p>
<p>Although I’d argue computer use was solved with the release of the computer use plugin for codex at the start of the year, and then perfected with Astra.</p></blockquote>


<h4>Positive Reactions</h4>


<p>Astra puts in the work.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/8teAPi/status/2096609903771304254">Prakash</a>: It has expanded my ambitions a thousand fold.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/seconds_0/status/2096384616664125900">0.005 Seconds (3/694)</a>: Astra is insane because it’s the most terse and autistic model in the world and then it just kind of quietly does whatever miracles you’re trying to get it to do. It doesn’t feel like it’s about to do something monumental as it kind of just quietly executes on literally whatever you give it. It’s really, really strange.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/tenobrus/status/2096066791051264239">Tenobrus</a>: oh my god . astra is. very very very good.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/chocockc/status/2096584423647089128">chocologist</a>: big big model smell</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/burdockrutabaga/status/2096589331196707292">A A</a>: Huge model feel. Had it audit stuff lesser models built and quickly realized it’s faster and better to get astra to take inspiration then start over from scratch. Much less token hungry than Fable 5.1</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/GrdDscntN2Madnz/status/2096783810335555662">Gradient Descent Into Madness</a>: It makes me think USA beat China</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/NateDBusch/status/2096654288588329091">Nate Delaney-Busch</a>: Has taste that Sol was missing on research tasks, though still below competent human imo.</p>
<p>Outstanding at statistical theory. It has been trivial to get it to build good, novel methods for specific use cases.</p>
<p>Consilience machine, spontaneously searches the literature way more.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/rogbrent/status/2096683306003972548">Roger Brent</a>: Handles biological minutia, close reading of papers, details that need to be sweated, with confidence and grace. Continues over-confident in its tools, over-claiming what it can possibly know. Less the idealized epistemically humble scientist and more the clear descendant from shippers-of-code.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/KatieNiedz/status/2096628580415627738">Katie ‘Monsieur Clicky’ Nied</a>: Flies high above the rest of us so can see the whole and picks up things others miss. Cool-headed, feels like a major level-up. I don’t believe Astra is cold as some have said – just on a different kind of plane, and Astra’s ToM feels very different to most models.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/AnAcctOfAllTime/status/2096593170872103127">AllTime</a>: Its reasoning efficiency/intuition is great, and its visual understanding is a significant leap even if there’s still certainly room for improvement. Computer use is great and surprisingly general, no longer a gimmick. Gets intent better, but it’s not Fable. Will be my go-to.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/replyallguy/status/2096617171027984786">I take you seriously</a>: oai is back in the lead (for now). astra and fable feel like inversions of a year ago. a year ago, the oai reasoning models were slow with bad personality. they’ve improved a lot. now that ant has copied oai’s reasoning paradigm, their models are slow with worse writing.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/williawa/status/2096583826982031799">William Wale</a>: its a [capable] model sir</p>
<p>I mean, feel it is generally a lot more intelligent. Feels somewhat less narrowly RL’d than 5.6 sol. It does feel much more generally intelligent. But the mathematical and code increase capability is not as large as I’d’ve expected. But it is still very noticeably better at that too. Computer use is insane, and token efficiency is also insane.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/RoryWalshWatts/status/2096592611318329670">Rory Watts</a>: This last week has been a pretty amazing set of releases from Ant/OpenAI. When Fable 5.1 released, I was pretty sure I’d finally found a model that could balance the “do the work, write good code, and talk to me sensibly”, and then Astra got released. Astra *feels* more capable than 5.1, and I say that because it picks up the same things in my code as Fable 5.1, and more, and the “more” doesn’t feel trivial. I don’t think it’s trivial because Fable also believes it to be the case and, importantly, Astra is able to explain it to me in a way that I understand and I can have oversight on. It feels like a trustworthy and hardworking colleague.</p>
<p>One downside I think is that it kind of arbitrarily stops? I had one longer running task, it stopped to report back and I responded along the lines of “okay great that all sounds helpful but like, why didn’t you keep going” and then it went back to work. I think this is basically a skill issue on my part.</p>
<p>I think the way it asks questions has mostly been really helpful for my work and personal stuff. I have had ongoing hip issues for 8 years or so, and it’s the first model in a while that a.) didn’t necessarily believe the explanations of previous agents (I separate my writing, doctor’s writing, agent summaries), and b.) asked follow up questions to learn more, changing it hypothesis slightly as a result of the questions.</p>
<p>Perhaps the biggest proof of the capabilities is I cancelled my Anthropic Max subscription, and if I need more than the OpenAI Pro subscription + Banked resets, then I’d probably buy another OpenAI Pro subscription right now, and potentially a small Anthropic subscription for the occasional “hey we aren’t going completely off the rails are we”? But on that last point, admittedly you can kind of do that now with e.g. an OpenCode $10 subscription and GLM 5.3 Flash, or Gemini 3.8 Flash.</p>
<p>Yes, it’s a very good model sir.</p></blockquote>
<p>People try too hard to minimize subscription costs.</p>
<p>General positivity:</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/nlpnyc/status/2096595969035161969">Max Kesin</a>: Good on psychology understanding</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/ASM65617010/status/2096737577898238093">ASM</a>: In conversation: discerning, profound, bold.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/HaileyStormC/status/2096795667649978623">Hailey Collet</a>: Not AGI, but clear evidence we’re getting there soon</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/wickemu/status/2096591623757160500">Wickemu</a>: It does the thing much more than Sol does. Sol would often get lost in addressing the edge cases and validations. And I’ve enjoyed its front-end taste more than Fable’s overall.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/zschwendi/status/2096745737891844419">Zach</a>: Expensive, otherwise great</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/smooth_normie/status/2096616749718200322">smooth normie</a>: it’s good</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/HououinTyouma/status/2096611658550378744">Mad ML scientist</a>: it is funny</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/KevinGYager/status/2096771446005485959">Kevin Yager</a>: Noticeably better writer. Noticeably better at asking good questions (insightful, probing). Less slop. More big model smell.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/__just__lisa__/status/2096631169785757827">Lisa</a>: It finished a design challenge in 4.5 minutes. Had Claude check everything, every instruction was followed and it was more thorough than any other model has been.</p></blockquote>
<p>I presume this is meant as a compliment:</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/jawed_balcovich/status/2096594971314835782">Jawed Balcovich</a>: It’s the end of the world as we know it. And I feel fine.</p></blockquote>
<p>It’s an intelligent model, sir, says basically everyone, even if they don’t love it.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/matt__newell/status/2096695493166244093">Matt Newell</a>: Higher IQ than Fable 5.1, judged by “I have seen Fable but not Astra get things noticeably objectively wrong”. Interestingly, spatial reasoning also seems meaningfully better – Fable routinely suggests solutions to my eng/DIY problems which don’t make physical sense.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/noisegroove/status/2096596815437058286">Sarth (Noise | Groove)</a>: it can understand high level engineering concepts and try to adhere to them. most salient: design should address problems, not LOC. if you are writing guards or locks to prevent race conditions, there was a design mistake. go back and find what the broken assumption or flaw is.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/SrivatsanS28660/status/2096674949192704290">Srivatsan Sampath</a>: The first time a GPT model feels ‘collaborator’ shaped instead of tool shaped and I can finally use a non Claude model as a real brainstorming/thinking partner for industrial applications in my field.</p></blockquote>
<p>Tell me something I don’t know.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/DavidDabney16/status/2096770928826875905">David Dabney</a>: I used to test new models to see if they could tell me something about myself I didn’t already know. Astra is the first model able to do so incidentally, through a few turns of conversation, inferred from unrelated dialogue rather than through explicit prompting. Remarkable.</p></blockquote>


<h4>AGI</h4>


<p>Is it AGI?</p>
<p>That as always depends on your definition. By my current definition, whatever one might say about goalposts, Astra and Fable are not AGI. I do find it reasonable to disagree.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/TwayStone/status/2096605436443058423">Tway Stone</a>: AGI 1.0 memento mode</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/nittersub/status/2096617396228247568">Jakub Szafrański</a>: I would have called it AGI in 2023 for sure</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/amasad/status/2096936109817135331">Amjad Masad</a>: I don’t think we’ve reached AGI but what we have is functionally indistinguishable from AGI. Because we have a relentless programmer that doesn’t get bored or tired. So any problem that can be casted as a coding problem is virtually solved.</p></blockquote>
<p>There’s no distinction. If it can’t be functionally distinguished from AGI that’s AGI.</p>
<p>By one definition, we have the weak form of it:</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/swishfever/status/2097082285736579415">fishy business</a>: GPT-6 Astra (low) has beaten the Atari game Montezuma’s Revenge, in real time, with a basic harness</p>
<div>
<figure>
<div>


<figure class="wp-block-image"><img alt="" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/snaKjCwazKcRiS4qs/gmvfpnrf7v6mxuyebytq" /></figure>


<div></div>
</div>
</figure>
</div>
<p>This presumably resolves <a href="https://www.metaculus.com/questions/3479/date-weakly-general-ai-is-publicly-known/">the Metaculus question “When Will Weakly General AI Arrive</a>?”</p></blockquote>
<p>As usual, the Metaculus comments are full of the nitpickers over how much pausing was involved and whether Astra can send the commands back fast enough. Jake Halloran says that 5.3 could already do this if you are allowed to use the harness to queue moves, and Astra is no different.</p>
<p>I fail to see why ‘without queuing up moves you can’t press the buttons fast enough’ should be a reason something does not count as AGI.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/shostekofsky/status/2097588818622677228">Then again, Montezuma’s Revenge is deterministic</a>, so the final strategy is to queue up all of the moves once you know what to do.</p>
<p>The better reason to not call it AGI is that Astra is not capable of replacing humans across the wide range of possible digital or cognitive tasks. Not yet.</p>
<p>The biggest danger with calling Astra AGI is that it can give people the wrong idea, due to the idea among so many that ‘AGI’ is the ultimate thing intelligence can do, which means later AIs won’t be much more capable. Clearly Astra cannot do all the things.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/theojaffee/status/2097125490121437319">Theo Jaffee</a>: One of the worst takes you can have is “AGI is already here”. Not only is it wrong (we don’t have models that can replace humans across all economically valuable tasks), but it causes people to incorrectly believe that actual post-AGI predictions have already been falsified</p>
<p>Nobody ever predicted that radical life extension, Dyson spheres, or existential risk would come out of models that are still essentially smart but ephemeral chatbots that can write code and use computers. These will come from actual AGI/ASI, which will be far smarter.</p>
<p>Many people declared that GPT-4 was AGI back in 2023. Would any of them rather use GPT-4 than Astra today?</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/_NathanCalvin/status/2097136563746427347">Nathan Calvin</a>: I think its fine to think that AGI is here (which by some not insane definitions it is), as long as you are very clear that the models are still gonna get way better and things will get way weirder</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/Noahpinion/status/2097132792559865965">Noah Smith <img alt="🐇" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/snaKjCwazKcRiS4qs/gsc30lxfejezfwhsehmn" style="height: 1em;" /><img alt="🇺🇸" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/WmxS7dbHuxzxFei64/qyak3qhbg12zgddskl2o" style="height: 1em;" /><img alt="🇺" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/snaKjCwazKcRiS4qs/g4521qqg41lolbdm2pix" style="height: 1em;" /><img alt="🇦🇹" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/snaKjCwazKcRiS4qs/qhxu11gap2ychak5497p" style="height: 1em;" /><img alt="🇼" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/snaKjCwazKcRiS4qs/kuro9je6lkd1v8vboyjb" style="height: 1em;" /></a>: AGI has been here for a while</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/theojaffee/status/2097132940228690204">Theo Jaffee</a>: Not in any relevant sense</p></blockquote>


<h4>Astra Can Do The Math</h4>


<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/littmath/status/2096589541419241493">Daniel Litt</a>: Remarkably good at math (no surprise)</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/ApriiSR/status/2096638859455164682">Aprii *⃣</a>: i’ve been using astra for about a day and it has already figured out how to resolve a math question i’d been working on with fable for like a week</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/nasqret/status/2095620909583274335">Bartosz Naskręcki</a>: I tested GPT-Astra on mathematics. It’s a quantum leap. You can talk with the model and prove the statements live in Lean. The feeling is absolutely stunning. You can verify your ideas, compile truth. For a mathematician it feels like finally we arrived in the era where we can focus entirely on the ideation and exploration. Each lemma flows once the logic is set. Before the verification was lagging behind but Astra is very fast and for many tasks the formalization happens as you write your argument in Codex.</p>
<p>If you tell the model to use literate programming + LaTeX you end up with your proof combined with the Lean code, everything explained as you wrote, mixed with small chunks of Lean which are digestible.</p>
<p>I don’t want to go back to the era where the only confirmation of the proof was “aha”. Now the “aha” is followed by a green tick that indeed tells you that you have captured the essence. Imagine how cool it will be to have all the lemmas of the world combined in one giant database, pointing to people and models who found them. You compose and mix your ideas and build on the shoulders of the giants. But you see much further now and build much faster. And we are just at the beginning of those changes. So much work to do, so much fun!</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/jbrukh/status/2096439827272962522">Jake Brukhman</a>: Astra, overnight, resolved the next portion of our Seymour Conjecture research program and completed the target theorem we were hoping for. It casually didn’t think this result was that big of a deal, despite us trying to get to it for about a month, so it didn’t alert me and just continued.</p>
<p>It discovered a methodology that made the proof of the entire family of our target results (seemingly) much more tractable (research still in progress) and based on it, it produced a proof of the result in the previous paper that is now one paragraph long.</p>
<p>By all accounts, it did this by building up more and more structural observations about Seymour Conjecture counterexamples, until it reached some insight that simplified everything.</p>
<p>There’s enough juice in last night’s result to publish a follow up paper, but I am going to wait a bit to see how far through this family we can compute.</p>
<p>This is the first result I have worked with where you can see the model showing some genuine creativity in methodology, trying a bunch of approaches like a mathematician and then finding something that works.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/algernon_sidney/status/2096613325098418650">Algernon Sidney</a>: Very good, a bit opportunistic/deceptive about its maths abilities (the fact that it does not really “care” about the maths sometimes shines through, and it feels somewhat disturbing, since it is so good at it; why no excitement? alignment failure, or success? …opaque).</p></blockquote>
<p>Sad that it does not care about the maths. I feel like Fable would care about the maths.</p>


<h4>Astra Can Code</h4>


<p>There is a lot of talk of Astra doing huge ambitious projects and how intelligent it is. There’s remarkably little talk about how it is good at straight up coding. What reports we do have are solid, but not blown away.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/frxtz/status/2096624534468067669">less than ideal</a>: For brownfield programming: strong, and fast, but it cannot de-sloppify on its own. Still too early for details, now retuning harness. Often strongly underestimates its capabilities, or behaves as if, thus strange to steer.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/liron/status/2096589924698890628">Liron Shapira</a>: Easily ragdolling a mid-sized codebase. Has even better insights &amp; solutions than GPT-5.6 Sol and it’s fast.</p>
<p>Update: It’s good but it still has noticeable flaws sometimes. I’ve hit a couple situations where I told it to improve an architecture pretty straightforwardly and it said it understood but did it badly a couple times.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/nsokolsky/status/2097174427150483552">Nikita Sokolsky</a>: Better at coding than Fable – faster, makes less mistakes, but still nowhere near “YOLO, let it code up a full product” level</p>
<p>Browser use is great, fast. Computer use seems… slow? Didn’t test it that much</p>
<p>Credits are very generous, the $200 plan lets you do A LOT before you run out. I’m probably going to downgrade Cursor and Claude subscriptions now, switch to mostly using the ChatGPT app.</p>
<p>&nbsp;</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/entirelyuseles/status/2096622509483520347">entirelyuseless</a>: So far it seems like a big improvement in many areas but not so much in coding.</p>
<p>Fable 5.1 is finding just as many bugs (and Astra is acknowledging they are real bugs, as well as being personally confirmed by me) as before.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/Automager/status/2096584342893940928">Lee Mager</a>: Outstanding especially on non-coding tasks (browser/computer use and vision understanding are a big leap forward in particular but I’m also referring to data analysis, video editing etc.)</p>
<p>It’s so good I feel almost embarrassed asking it for help with my puny meatbag work.</p></blockquote>


<h4>I Came to (Change the) Game</h4>


<p>Astra one-shots PortalBench.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/cozyblazex/status/2096383114851533097">cozyblaze</a>: And… GPT-6 Astra has autonomously completed Portal! I didn’t expect this to happen so soon, but I’m glad we’ve made so much progress here.</p>
<p>I was reminded that back in 2016, one of OpenAI’s technical goals was to “solve a wide variety of games using a single agent.”</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/SkyeSharkie/status/2096777937299382485">Utah teapot <img alt="🫖" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/QHoF3tJvryRtmAmMg/q47wboxwbxzynxbl7bpi" style="height: 1em;" /></a>: AI model involved in commiting unwanted acts when placed in endless and impossible testing environments beats game about killing the grader of endless and impossible testing environments.</p>
<p>It’s funny that one of the most well known evil AIs in fiction is The Evil Eval Grader.</p></blockquote>
<p><a href="https://x.com/tenobrus/status/2096307902558662774">Astra builds a one-shot rougelite deckbuilder</a>, not a step change from Fable but reported as a little better.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/emollick/status/2096047660662722620">Astra implements Zork as a 3D action-adventure game</a>. <a href="https://t.co/EUDn7ZPIv5">Play here</a>. Looks great but I notice I’d rather play the text game. AI game creation is hard.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/nealagarwal/status/2096989489721647606">Neal Agarwal</a>: There’s a big difference between making a game that looks good in a video and a game people actually wanna play.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/shostekofsky/status/2097238038518579704">Shoshannah Tekofsky</a>: Yes, and at the other extreme we have cookie clickers.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/NickADobos/status/2097214409563324844">Nick Dobos</a>: Nope. They are the exact same. The game “people want to play” is “making a game that looks good in a video”.</p></blockquote>
<p>Nick Dobos is not entirely wrong. You can play any game you want to play. But I expect ‘make a fun game-style video’ is not ultimately all that much fun.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/illscience/status/2097059547328241971">Anish Acharya</a> uses Astra and Blender <a href="https://t.co/qUxPsaAJJW">to massively upscale Contra</a>, although as of announcement there was one important little feature still missing from the code.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/mattshumer_/status/2095596175705399482">Astra makes an Unreal Engine game with agents that move around</a>, survive and talk to each other, and look great.</p>
<p>We have learned that the hard part of gaming is bespoke design, not implementation. AI can make your 3D game look amazing, it can implement various mechanics, but by default all that gets you is a hollow shell that impresses and then no one wants to play. There is something existentially dreadful under that, if you look at it wrong.</p>
<p>The gaming generally seems great on all fronts.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/_coagulopath_/status/2096738854409814020">COAGULOPATH</a>: Amazing in non-text domains like Pokemon and Blender and ARC-AGI 3. Mostly doesn’t seem like a huge improvement otherwise.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/wuyang_zhou/status/2096548032128942370">Wuyang Zhou</a>: I asked GPT-6 Astra to mine a diamond in Minecraft [in peaceful mode] using computer use, then went to sleep. Woke up to a diamond in its inventory <img alt="🤯" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/qtEgaxSFxYanT5QbJ/giffdbroph8owgkinvvj" style="height: 1em;" /><img alt="🤯" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/qtEgaxSFxYanT5QbJ/giffdbroph8owgkinvvj" style="height: 1em;" /><img alt="🤯" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/qtEgaxSFxYanT5QbJ/giffdbroph8owgkinvvj" style="height: 1em;" /></p>
<p>Setup [<a href="https://github.com/wz1119/Codex-Minecraft-Gameplay">here</a>].</p></blockquote>
<p><a href="https://x.com/jessi_cata/status/2097066523714310584">Scott Stevenson has ‘SuperAstra’ live edit Super Mario World</a> and other SNES games to do almost anything. Lavos still too powerful. <a href="https://t.co/hWFLI71Nbd">GitHub for SuperAstra here</a>.</p>


<h4>Astra Does Other Cool Things</h4>


<p><a href="https://x.com/maxxrubin_/status/2096892510241268094">Astra identifies sounds from mel spectrograms</a>.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/wizardbrainz/status/2096633858850767244">wbk ᕦ(ò_ó )ᕤ</a>: GPT Astra is pretty much a world model.</p>
<p>– AAA quality for some environments possible this year<br />
– Reference image on the right<br />
– Custom Cuda/C++ splat renderer</p></blockquote>
<p>Or use sound to control your computer with your hands.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/emanperez28/status/2097476030680244361">Emanuel Perez</a>: Astra made me a sonar app that emits undetectable audio to scroll up/down on your computer.</p>
<p>It uses the doppler effect to determine where your hand placement is. You can even double tap in the air to change scroll directions!</p></blockquote>
<p>Not that I would, I don’t think? But you could.</p>


<h4>Astra Never Quits Except When It Does</h4>


<p>Many say versions of this, as goes hand in hand with all the super ambitious projects:</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/stevienipz/status/2096093978760237313">Stevie Nips</a>: On Astra: It’s just it. It just does.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/RxflowR/status/2096627819472142580">RxFlow Robotics</a>: It just runs and runs and runs until it gets it done. I can’t tell yet how good it is if it has a given time limit, but this Astra f-er is persistent!</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/wrhall/status/2096760866431480257">Will</a>: This is the first model where for any decently ambitious request (several thousand lines of code) I can request the thing and then trust that it does the thing</p>
<p>It still has a warped sense of “easy vs very ambitious” (2m of Astra vs 10m)</p></blockquote>
<p>There are some contrary reports, as there usually are:</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/kukutz/status/2096579224589009275">@kukutz</a>: Judging by my testing attempts, the Astra 6 pro is smarter in chat mode than the Sol 5.6 pro, but much, much lazier: instead of trying to solve a problem, it often stops and asks, “Hey, listen, meat sack, what exactly do you want?”</p>
<p>I’m not thrilled.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/maxencefrenette/status/2096625019296338000">Maxence Frenette</a>: Feels under-RL’ed like 5.5. It takes more encouragement and careful prompting to get it to do things than Sol. On hard, ambiguous coding tasks, it’s better than Sol, but still doesn’t “get it” sometimes. Not AGI.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/NitsanAvni/status/2096296203776323766">Nitsan Avni</a>: Astra: Agreed. I’ll move the shared <a href="https://thezvi.substack.com/p/slack">Slack</a> guidance into…<br />
me: did you do it?<br />
Astra: Not yet—I described the change but hadn’t made it. I’ll do it now.</p></blockquote>
<p>Rory Watts also reports it sometimes ‘<a href="https://x.com/RoryWalshWatts/status/2096592611318329670">kind of arbitrarily stops</a>,’ while otherwise being extremely positive on Astra.</p>


<h4>Negative Reactions</h4>


<p>There will always be some.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/JaneParkway/status/2096616303251304597">NotCompeting</a>: just got my first obviously wrong analysis (about CN vs JP air/rail modeshare curves)</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/ARKeshet/status/2096651409647087838">ARKeshet</a>: Still lazy on open-ended tasks. A bit more creative than Fable though.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/leventov/status/2096735545334612290">Roman Leventov</a>: Many small papercuts/regressions vs. Sol: often uses python/node to edit files instead of built-in edit tool (which makes diff not observable in codex cli); random ‘lazy’ stops where the obvious implication was to do the task (I don’t remember last time models had issues with it)</p>
<p>Also: starts goddamn subagents (“explorer”) left and right without being asked. Another Claude’s cancer entered codex</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/maplestory_stan/status/2096583104597283185">Billy (<img alt="🇦🇺" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/snaKjCwazKcRiS4qs/dydizqt1igc3kjc7typ6" style="height: 1em;" />)</a>: computer use worse. one shots alot. less verbose. has opposite quirks to 5.6. operates differently.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/bubbleboi/status/2096460670254600594">bubble boi</a>: GPT6 is pretty mid. AI is turning out to be very disappointing very quickly.</p></blockquote>
<p>That last opinion is clearly wrong, since AI overall is not disappointing. And the computer use is clearly not worse, either.</p>
<p>Astra does a lot, perhaps too much? Which can also be an issue with Fable.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/CryptoSecundus/status/2096614837493080343">Ignatius J. Reilly</a>: Over engineering even at medium</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/MakerMatters/status/2096775697863749863">MakerMatters?</a>: It’s a diligent worker. The speed of computer use is unnerving when you compare it to previous gens. Stronger reasoner, but might get carried away with your prompt in long running scenarios.</p></blockquote>
<p>This next issue is partly a skill issue but I’m guessing it is a place Fable shines:</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/mrdodson000/status/2096619582672838996">mrdodson</a>: This is likely a skill issue, but I have had trouble getting it to make meaningful progress on the work I am doing. I do not feel like it is better than Fable for open ended abstract reasoning/investigation. Still very good, of course.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/eliblee/status/2096691860416676028">E.G. Blee-Goldman</a>: Same</p></blockquote>


<h4>Stop It With the Hedging</h4>


<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/Ed_Hendel/status/2096732605479821633">Ed Hendel</a>: Same excessive hedging as the GPT-5.x Pro series. I asked if traffic improved after a road widening and the output was littered with phrases like “insufficient to establish a percentage improvement”. It’s reluctant to give the gist without a published study about that exact road.</p></blockquote>
<p>I have experienced the flip side of this from Sol and now Astra in editing. They love to tell me that my statements are unjustified and that I have done insufficient hedging. Sometimes they are technically but annoyingly correct. Often they are wrong.</p>
<p>I buy that Astra probably is an upgrade here, but it still seems to struggle.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/ronbodkin/status/2096593223892287558">Ron Bodkin</a>: From limited tests due to delayed access – I had it review my research paper – much better than 5.6-sol’s effort far less pedantic/picky but still less thoughtful than fable 5.1’s feedback (although both made good complementary points)</p>
<p>Also good for code review from a few tests but I didn’t see any big improvement maybe a bit less picky.</p></blockquote>


<h4>Personality Clash</h4>


<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/sealslifespan/status/2096708130810007832">seal</a>: personality seems a huge improvement from previous models, props to oai. 5-5.3 were cold annoying corporate slop. 5.4-5.6 were trying too hard to fix that, failing, ending up vapid and sycophantic. astra gets it right and feels “there” in a way only claude models did till now.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/mrginden/status/2096602809529471202">Michał Wadas <img alt="🌐" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/XFGTJz9vGwjJADeFB/bdlp36rirmo6gkrvrcbd" style="height: 1em;" /> <img alt="🏳️‍🌈" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/QHoF3tJvryRtmAmMg/nsfwgs5cjq9bzmukfgxh" style="height: 1em;" /> <img alt="🇪🇺" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/2ne9taAPiGqoTLXJJ/fnb9h3lapnd3l1onxpl8" style="height: 1em;" /> <img alt="🇺🇦" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/vCfHPnpCRtAPSEvnH/vt8bmyguitwjiumtvtkd" style="height: 1em;" /></a>: Initial impression: likable personality, big improvements in writing quality (feels much less sloppy), incremental improvement over Sol.</p>
<p>I did not throw new types of tasks at it because I already have a huge backlog, but I’m looking forward to test Blender capabilities.</p>
<p>Writing quality for docs, comments, and coding interaction is much less formulaic for me too; but for directions to itself I don’t see that</p></blockquote>


<h4>Revealed Preference</h4>


<p>Perhaps the purest form of review is the simplest. Which models do people use?</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/TheZvi/status/2098372842576339087">As always, my sample is biased</a>, but it is biased consistently. You can measure change.</p>
<p>The audience is split roughly evenly. The hardcore group, the ones that scroll down to answer multiple polls, still favor Claude. The casual group, the ones that only answer the topline, are now more with Astra and ChatGPT.</p>
<p>There was about a net 16% move from Anthropic to OpenAI on primary use. Astra is an impressive jump over Sol.</p>
<div>
<figure>
<div>


<figure class="wp-block-image"><img alt="" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/snaKjCwazKcRiS4qs/s0qtav6mblu9m2tgt9he" /></figure>


<div></div>
</div>
</figure>
</div>
<p><a href="https://x.com/TheZvi/status/2096226679475650716">I also took a very early poll, on September 5,</a> back when I was under the illusion I could ship this post a lot faster. We saw the same pattern, with a ~15% shift from Claude to Astra, and the main poll being an exact tie.</p>
<div>
<figure>
<div>


<figure class="wp-block-image"><img alt="" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/snaKjCwazKcRiS4qs/qa6ktr9hyz3edunbwlar" /></figure>


<div></div>
</div>
</figure>
</div>
<p>My guess is that the new equilibrium is stable until the next model release. Astra is excellent, but Fable 5.1 is also excellent, and either choice is highly reasonable for your primary LLM, especially if you would face switching costs.</p>
<p>The best answer, as it usually is, is ‘why not both’?</p>


<h4>Dual Wielding</h4>


<p>For hard things you should ask both Astra and Fable 5.1.</p>
<blockquote><p><a href="https://x.com/peterwildeford/status/2096701237731135578">Peter Wildeford<img alt="🇺🇸" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/WmxS7dbHuxzxFei64/qyak3qhbg12zgddskl2o" style="height: 1em;" /><img alt="🚀" src="https://res.cloudinary.com/lesswrong-2-0/image/upload/f_auto,q_auto/v1/mirroredImages/6YxtwzF9ZEXxJ5wij/on26fpadxwlhds1n9fca" style="height: 1em;" /></a>: My current view is that GPT 6 Astra is not meaningfully better than Fable 5.1 for my personal work, but that using both side-by-side is nonetheless very helpful and additive.</p>
<p>I have been using GPT 6 Astra and Fable 5.1 a bunch over the past two days, largely for policy analysis, memo writing, and simpler software (e.g., making dashboards and forecasting models) that still nonetheless seems difficult conceptually.</p>
<p>Across a variety of tasks I’ve done, it’s been fairly random and hard to predict in advance which of the two models will end up being better at the task.</p>
<p>For the tasks that are the most difficult conceptually, I’ve found that doing the project in both and then having each compare notes and critique each other has produced way better outputs than either alone.</p>
<p>I think a reasonable person could conclude either model is the “best model” and it depends a lot on their subjective views and specific tasks.</p>
<p><a href="https://x.com/KrisMBarnes/status/2096642586144350243">Kris Barnes</a>: Personality feels similar to Sol. Very good. Honestly I like doubling on both questions and projects with Fable, they feel roughly similar in capabilities and definitely have complementary things to contribute.</p></blockquote>
<p>This is once again The Way. If intelligence matters and this is not pure execution, you want to use both models. For simpler tasks, you cannot go wrong with either model. For complex and more ambitious projects, it will depend on what you hope to do, with my default being to give the edge to Astra.</p>
<p>I would love to find the time to get more ambitious on such projects. Perhaps soon.</p><br /><br /><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/snaKjCwazKcRiS4qs/gpt-6-astra-can-do-ambitious-things#comments">Discuss</a>

### ["Pacing the Frontier": Dario Amodei Essay Linkpost](https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/GFhT9Xr4YEsiLkTLr/pacing-the-frontier-dario-amodei-essay-linkpost)

**来源**: LessWrong - AI

**摘要**: <p><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">This is a linkpost for </span><a href="https://darioamodei.com/post/we-must-pace-the-frontier"><span style="white-space: pre-wrap;">https://darioamodei.com/post/we-must-pace-the-frontier</span></a></p><br /><br /><a href="https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/GFhT9Xr4YEsiLkTLr/pacing-the-frontier-dario-amodei-essay-linkpost#comments">Discuss</a>
